   - `mvp2_rsft_train_r1` (PREPARING) — RSFT training about to start, pass criteria: rel_f1 > 0.4057 AND evi_f1 > 0.798

2. **Report** is stale — still shows s0 as "failed" in near-recent conclusions, doesn't reflect the CED scoring completion or RSFT train preparation. 

3. All 4 GPUs are currently idle with no active experiments running. The worker `worker_ssh_urgent-shard-status_20260515T043257` failed, and based on its name and timestamp (04:32:57 UTC on May 15), it was monitoring the shard generation status while s1/s2 were wrapping up around 04:34-04:37 UTC and s0 was still in progress.

From the s0 notes, this worker was CED-scoring the partial output from shard 0 — specifically 21,728 NLI records on GPU 0 — when the generation hit 84.6% progress and received a SIGTERM signal. The partial shard covered about 640 out of 757 documents, giving combined 4-shard coverage of roughly 96%, and the decision was made to proceed with the partial shard 0 despite the interruption.

However, after the restart, the s0 notes were updated to show that generation actually completed fully with all 682 documents (100%), and the SIGTERM was just a normal cleanup signal after successful completion. The format metrics look good at 99.6% format_ok, 10.1% truncation rate, and 7.6/8 diversity. So the Agent already corrected the notes and launched CED scoring through other workers, which completed successfully.

Since the CED scoring and merge are already done, this urgent-shard-status worker failure is likely just a historical artifact that's been superseded by the later successful work. I should verify whether this failure actually matters or if it's already been handled.

But we already have the per-shard scoring completed and merged (rsft_scored_s{0-3} → rsft_filtered with 2718 samples).
 
Now I'm checking the timeline: the rsft_filtered output was created at 13:22, but this process has been running since at least 12:55 based on the cmdline file timestamp. That means this PID 159689 process is likely the one that actually generated the rsft_filtered directory, not the merge worker.

Looking at what this process does, it takes the entire `/root/autodl-tmp/rsft_generations` directory as input and outputs directly to `/root/autodl-tmp/rsft_filtered`, which would process all shards at once. But the per-shard scoring created separate rsft_scored_s{0-3} directories that were then merged into rsft_filtered with 2718 samples. So there's a potential conflict here: either the per-shard scoring pipeline created rsft_filtered through merging, or this long-running process is overwriting it with its own full-directory scoring.

The timeline is starting to make sense though — PID 159689 was launched around 12:55 to score all generations at once, while the CED scoring workers independently scored each shard from 12:42-13:19, and then the merge worker concatenated those per-shard results at 13:22. If PID 159689 is still running and completes, it could overwrite the merged data with its own output.

The concerning part is that the worker status shows "failed" — so the worker that spawned this process failed, but the process itself is still running on the GPU as a leftover. I already checked rsft_filtered earlier and found it contains the merge output (the combined report with per-shard breakdowns matches the individual results), so PID 159689 completing would be destructive.

Let me think through what the rsft_score_filter script is actually doing with `--selection_strategy top_k --top_k 1`. It's processing the entire input_dir, which includes the original generations.jsonl file with 21968 lines from before sharding — that's different content than what's in the per-shard files, so the results could diverge. I need to check if this process is still actively working or just sitting idle.

When it completes, it will write output to `--output_path /root/autodl-tmp/rsft_filtered`, which will **overwrite** the already-merged training data!

The current rsft_filtered data:
- rsft_train.jsonl: 2718 samples (from per-shard scoring + merge)


- rsft_combined_report.json: merge report

If PID 159689 finishes, it will overwrite with its own results which process the whole generations.jsonl (including potentially different records, as the root generations.jsonl may include data from the failed pre-shard run).

This is **severity: critical** — an orphan process is about to corrupt the training data.

Let me trace through what the process actually reads. It's using `--input_dir /root/autodl-tmp/rsft_generations`, which contains the root generations.jsonl file (21968 lines from the pre-shard merge) plus the individual shard directories with their own generations.jsonl files.

The process reports 21728 records after dedup, which matches reading just the root file (21968 → 21728 after dedup), not all files combined. So it's processing the merged file as a single batch rather than the per-shard approach, which could introduce subtle differences in the final results.

Looking at the dedup numbers more carefully, the root file removed 240 duplicates while the per-shard approach only removed 16 total, suggesting there are significant cross-shard duplicates that the whole-file approach catches but the per-shard method misses. This could actually make the single-process scoring cleaner overall.

But the immediate problem is that the rsft_score_filter script will overwrite everything in that output directory when it finishes, which means I need to alert Director right away about this collision risk.
```

Since these evaluations are on a limited subset of 50 documents, they're inherently noisy, so the real validation will come from running the full dev set. For my review, I need to confirm s44 completed successfully, flag that we need the full dev eval to actually meet the pass criteria, note the unregistered rsft_score_filter process with top_k=3, and verify GPU compliance.

```text
This is a critical finding. The eval worker for s44 seems to have completed, and the Agent has already updated r1 with eval results. Let me look at the r1 metrics:

**mvp2_rsft_train_r1 (DONE, conclusion: negative)**:
- rel_f1: 0.0483 ← **catastrophic drop** from SFT baseline 0.4057
- ign_f1: 0.0483
- evi_f1: 0.7646 ← below SFT baseline 0.798
- edcr: 0.6534 ← below SFT baseline 0.7118


- num_preds: 848 ← **only 848 predictions** vs SFT baseline 12234
- step170_rel_f1: 0.0458, step170_evi_f1: 0.7514

The worker notification was for eval-rsft-s44, but r1 already has complete dev eval metrics with a negative conclusion, which means the Agent finished the full evaluation run.

Looking at the r1 results, the performance is catastrophic — relation extraction F1 plummeted from 0.4057 down to 0.0483, an 88% relative drop, and the model's barely making any predictions at all with only 848 outputs compared to 12234 from the SFT baseline. This is the same devastating pattern I saw with GRPO, where the RSFT training essentially wiped out the model's relation extraction capability. The mvp2_eval_rsft_s44 is still preparing, so the worker probably finished r1 and kicked off s44. This is a critical failure — the RSFT approach with top_k=1 CED filtering has completely broken the model against the pass criteria.

But I need to pause and consider whether this is actually a training failure or an evaluation bug. The in-training metrics looked stable with eval_loss=0.762 and accuracy=0.829, which are close to SFT performance — so how could the model be completely broken if token accuracy is that high? The key might be in that num_preds number of 848. The model could be generating outputs that parse correctly at the token level but are extremely conservative about actually predicting relations, suggesting either the model learned to only predict when highly confident or the RSFT data distribution shifted in a way that caused the model to overfit to a different pattern.

Let me check the eval metrics for s44 separately to understand what's happening, and I should verify this with the server first.
```

_source line 423 · 2026-05-15T07:41:01.023Z_

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   I am acting as an independent scientific reviewer (Reviewer role) for the "faith-evidence-ie" ML research project. My responsibilities are:
   - Independently verify experiment conclusions using read-only tools (registry, SSH, W&B, logs)
   - Check GPU resource compliance (processes within project's gpu_ids whitelist)
   - Detect orphan/unregistered processes on GPU servers
   - Report findings to Director via `reviewer_post_to_director` with severity levels (info/warning/critical)
   - All output must be in Chinese
   - React to system notifications about experiment completions, failures, and worker completions
   - Proactively flag issues (data corruption risks, untracked processes, inaccurate registry data)

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **RSFT (Rejection Sampling Fine-Tuning)**: Generate N=8 candidates per training sample, CED-score them, select top_k best as training data
   - **CED (Calibrated Evidence Distance)**: NLI-based scoring using cross-encoder/nli-deberta-v3-base with tau threshold
   - **SFT Warmup**: Base fine-tuning on Qwen3-4B with QLoRA (rank=64, alpha=128), producing baseline rel_f1=0.4057, evi_f1=0.7979
   - **GRPO (Group Relative Policy Optimization)**: Previously attempted, catastrophic forgetting at all lr values (5e-5, 5e-6)
   - **Catastrophic Forgetting Pattern**: Both GRPO and RSFT destroy relation extraction capability while evidence metrics may improve
   - **4-shard parallel generation**: Generation split across 4 GPUs, each processing ~750 docs
   - **CED scoring strategies**: top_k=1 (12.4% selection, 2718 samples) vs top_pct=25% (24.9%) vs top_k=3 (~8000 samples ablation)
   - **Multi-seed variance estimation**: 3 seeds (42, 43, 44) for training stability verification
   - **DocRED dataset**: 3053 train docs, 985 dev docs, relation extraction + evidence task

3. Files and Code Sections:
   - **Registry (registry.yaml)**: Central experiment tracking. Key experiments reviewed:
     - mvp2_rsft_gen_s0/s1/s2/s3: Generation shards, all completed successfully
     - mvp2_rsft_ced_scoring: 21952 → 2718 selected (12.4%, top_k=1)
     - mvp2_rsft_train_r1: RSFT training seed=42, **conclusion=negative, rel_f1=0.0483**
     - mvp2_rsft_train_r1_s43/s44: Seeds 43/44 for variance, both completed
     - mvp2_eval_rsft_s44: Full dev eval, PREPARING
     - mvp2_rsft_ced_scoring_topk3: Ablation with top_k=3, PENDING
   
   - **Server paths verified**:
     - `/root/autodl-tmp/rsft_generations/shard_{0-3}/generations.jsonl` — generation outputs (5560/5552/5568/5288 lines)
     - `/root/autodl-tmp/rsft_generations/shard_{0-3}/log.txt` — actual process logs showing completion
     - `/root/autodl-tmp/rsft_scored_s{0-3}/` — per-shard CED scoring results
     - `/root/autodl-tmp/rsft_filtered/rsft_train.jsonl` — merged training data (2718 samples)
     - `/root/autodl-tmp/rsft_filtered/rsft_combined_report.json` — merge report confirming all shard numbers
     - `/root/autodl-tmp/rsft_output/` — r1 (seed=42) trained model
     - `/root/autodl-tmp/rsft_output_s43/` — seed=43 trained model
     - `/root/autodl-tmp/rsft_output_s44/` — seed=44 trained model
     - `~/runs/faith-evidence-ie/mvp2_rsft_train_r1.log` — training log
     - `/root/autodl-tmp/rsft_filtered_topk3/` — top_k=3 ablation output path
   
   - **report.md**: Stale throughout session — still showed s0 as "failed" and old alert list, never updated to reflect RSFT pipeline progress

4. Errors and Fixes:
   - **s0 false failure**: Registry marked s0 as failed (EXIT_CODE=143) but actual shard_0/log.txt showed 682/682 complete. Monitoring log captured SIGTERM of the tmux wrapper, not the Python process. Agent corrected to success.
   - **Inaccurate progress notes**: Agent notes claimed "~84.6% progress" and "~640/757 docs" for s0, but actual was 100% (682/682). Flagged as warning.
   - **Workers reporting done prematurely**: All CED scoring workers launched nohup background processes then exited with status=done. Actual scoring continued for 10-30+ minutes after worker completion. Flagged consistently.
   - **Orphan process PID 159689 (CRITICAL)**: urgent-shard-status worker (failed) left behind a rsft_score_filter process scoring 21728 records, outputting to same path as merged data. Would have overwritten rsft_train.jsonl (2718 valid samples). Flagged as critical, Director had it killed.
   - **Unregistered GPU processes**: Multiple instances of CED scoring processes not registered in registry. Flagged as warnings; Agent later registered mvp2_rsft_ced_scoring and mvp2_rsft_ced_scoring_topk3.
   - **RSFT catastrophic failure**: rel_f1 dropped from 0.4057 to 0.0483 (88% relative decrease), num_preds from 12234 to 848. Same catastrophic forgetting pattern as GRPO. Was about to post critical review when summary was requested.

5. Problem Solving:
   - **Verified data integrity across entire RSFT pipeline**: Generation → CED scoring → merge → training, independently confirming numbers at each stage
   - **Detected and prevented data corruption**: Orphan process would have overwritten training data; flagged in time for kill
   - **Cross-seed stability verified**: 3 seeds showed <0.04% variance in training metrics (eval_loss, accuracy)
   - **Selection strategy change identified**: Partial run used top_pct=25% (517 samples), full run used top_k=1 (2718 samples) — different approaches documented
   - **Truncation impact quantified**: ~8.8% truncation rate, truncated samples had 0.156pp lower f1, due to max_new_tokens=1024 limit
   - **Outstanding**: RSFT catastrophic failure requires root cause analysis — in-training metrics looked healthy (accuracy 0.829) but actual relation extraction collapsed (rel_f1=0.0483)

6. All User Messages:

7. Pending Tasks:
   - **Post critical review of RSFT eval results**: r1 rel_f1=0.0483 (catastrophic drop from 0.4057). Was mid-investigation when summary requested.
   - **Verify mvp2_eval_rsft_s44 results**: s44 eval is PREPARING/running (PID 174837 on GPU 2), need to check if results match r1's catastrophic pattern
   - **Monitor top_k=3 CED scoring ablation**: PID 173175 still running on GPU 0+3, not yet complete

   - **Root cause analysis**: Why does RSFT training produce healthy in-training metrics (accuracy 0.829) but catastrophic relation extraction (rel_f1=0.0483, only 848 predictions vs 12234)?

8. Current Work:
   I was reviewing the eval-rsft-s44 worker completion and discovered that the Agent had already populated r1 with full dev eval results showing **catastrophic failure**: rel_f1=0.0483 (vs SFT baseline 0.4057), conclusion=negative. I had identified this as a critical finding and checked GPU processes (PID 173175 for top_k3 scoring on GPU 0+3, PID 174837 likely s44 eval on GPU 2). I was about to formulate and post a critical review report to the Director analyzing:

Looking at the full picture, PID 173175 (top_k3 scoring) is split across GPU 0 and GPU 3 — the command specifies `--nli_device cuda:3` so the NLI model is on GPU 3 with 1488 MiB, while GPU 0 has another 500 MiB, likely from PyTorch's default memory allocation. Now I need to check what GPU whitelist is configured for this project to understand if these allocations are expected.

Let me shift focus to the main task though — I need to post the critical review about RSFT eval. Looking at the registry data, mvp2_rsft_train_r1 finished with negative results: the relative F1 dropped to 0.0483 compared to the baseline SFT's 0.4057, while evidence F1 stayed similar at 0.7646 versus 0.7979, and the number of predictions plummeted from 12234 to 848. The in-training metrics showed eval loss of 0.7624 and mean token accuracy of 0.8.

I'm tracking the other runs now — mvp2_rsft_train_r1_s43 is analyzing with training complete but no eval yet, s44 has training done with eval currently running on GPU 2 (PID 174837), and mvp2_eval_rsft_s44 is preparing. The critical pattern I'm seeing mirrors what happened with GRPO: RSFT r1 shows a catastrophic failure where relative F1 dropped 88% and the model essentially learned to suppress predictions entirely, cutting output by 93%.

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Current state:
1. **s44 eval**: 7/124 batches (~5.6%), ETA ~1.3 hours. Results not yet available.
2. **top_k3 scoring**: 6000/21728 records (~27.6%), about 19 min in. At ~350 records/min, remaining ~45 min.

Now I need to post the critical review about the RSFT r1 catastrophic failure. The key data points:

**RSFT r1 (seed=42) full dev eval**:


The model completely collapsed on relation extraction with rel_f1 dropping 88% to just 0.0483, while evidence extraction held relatively steady at 0.7646 (only 4.2% down). Most striking is the 93% plummet in num_preds from 12234 to 848, suggesting the model learned to suppress predictions entirely. It failed both pass criteria since rel_f1 needs to stay above 0.4057 and evi_f1 above 0.798.

This mirrors the catastrophic forgetting I saw with GRPO at different learning rates, though RSFT performs marginally better. The in-training metrics were deceptive—token accuracy of 0.829 masked the actual task collapse. Both reinforcement learning and rejection sampling approaches seem to hit a fundamental wall with this model-task combination, so the top_k=3 ablation probably won't salvage things.

Now I'm checking what roadmap adjustments need to happen given this RSFT failure.
```

exp_id: mvp2_grpo_ced_r1\\\\n    role: primary\\\\n    result: KILL — f1_reward 归零, 无 checkpoint, 训练不可挽回\\\\n  depends_on:\\\\n  - D005\\\\n- id: D007\\\\n  date: 2026-05-14 11:28 UTC\\\\n  summary: r3 回退 num_iterations=1，隔离变量逐一测试\\\\n  rationale: Reviewer CRITICAL 确认：r2 的 KL 在 step 5 爆到 37亿、step 15 爆到 3400亿，直接导致 loss\\\\n    爆炸+OOM。根因是 num_iterations=2 + QLoRA 4-bit 量化 logits 精度不足，两次迭代间 KL 被放大。r2 同时改了\\\\n    6 个超参，崩溃后无法归因。r3 必须回退 num_iterations=1，保留 reward weighting（reward 0.25→0.42 正常上升证明\\\\n    WeightedRewardFn 有效），一次只改一个变量。\\\\n  related_experiments:\\\\n  -    - rel_f1: 0.4057 → 0.0483 (88% relative drop)
   - evi_f1: 0.7979 → 0.7646 (both criteria failed)
   - num_preds: 12234 → 848 (93% drop)
   - Both pass criteria failed

3. **This mirrors the same catastrophic forgetting pattern I saw with GRPO**: Whether it's GRPO at different learning rates or RSFT with top_k=1, all these approaches consistently obliterate relation extraction performance while evidence metrics stay relatively stable or even tick up slightly.

4. **In-training metrics are deceiving me**: Token accuracy looked solid at 0.829, but the actual task performance completely collapsed — that's a crucial red flag about what's really happening during training.

5. **Checking GPU utilization**: GPU 2 is running the s44 eval (about halfway through), GPU 3 is scoring the top_k3 results (over a quarter done), and the other two are idle.

6. **Still waiting on**: The s44 eval to finish, s43 hasn't started yet, and top_k3 scoring has roughly 45 minutes left.

Now I need to write up the critical findings and send them to Director, starting with the RSFT catastrophic failure where relation F1 dropped to 0.0483.

The core issues are clear: RSFT completely failed the pass criteria, the in-training metrics gave false confidence, both GRPO and RSFT show the same catastrophic forgetting problem, and the roadmap is way out of date with what's actually happened.

What's particularly revealing is the prediction count difference — RSFT learned to suppress predictions heavily (848 vs SFT's 12234), whereas GRPO sometimes went the opposite direction with way more predictions but essentially zero precision. RSFT's strategy of fewer predictions with marginally better precision still isn't enough to salvage the results.

Let me work through the actual metrics here. The registry shows rel_f1=0.0483 for RSFT, and if I assume balanced precision and recall, that gives me roughly 41 true positives out of 848 predictions, which translates to a recall around 0.34% against the 12151 gold examples — meaning precision would be around 4.8%. The model is essentially broken in a different way than GRPO, but broken nonetheless.

Looking at the training progression, step 170 had rel_f1=0.0458 with evi_f1=0.7514, and given the training setup of 3 epochs over 2718 samples with batch size 16, that's roughly 510 total steps, so step 170 is about a third of the way through. The final checkpoint is marginally better, but the core issue remains: the model learned to drastically suppress its predictions, collapsing from 12234 down to 848.
